and we take any

\widetilde{R}>R+T+2C_{0}t.

With this choice, for any
\xi_{0}\in\partial\Omega\setminus B_{\widetilde{R}}(0)
, we have

I_{0}(\Delta(\xi_{0},2t))\subset\Delta(\xi_{0},2C_{0}t)\subset\partial\Omega\setminus B_{R+T}(0),

and therefore,

\sup_{x\in I_{0}(\Delta(\xi_{0},2t))}\|\nu\|_{*}(\Delta(x,2C_{0}t))\leq\sup_{x\in\partial\Omega\setminus B_{R+T}(0)}\|\nu\|_{*}(\Delta(x,T))\overset{\text{{(\ref{sup in far from ball of nu at T})}}}{\leq}C_{1}\delta,

and the conclusions in the Semmes decomposition
Theorem

4.4
hold (with
C_{1}\delta
) for all
x\in\partial\Omega\setminus B_{R+T}(0)\supset I_{0}(\Delta(\xi_{0},2t))
and all
0<r\leq 2C_{0}t
. This concludes the proof.
∎

5.
The double layer potential at large scales. Proof of
Theorem

3.5

This section is dedicated to the proof of
Theorem

3.5
. During this section we assume
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
as in the statement of
Theorem

3.5
. Briefly,
\Omega
is a
2
-sided CAD with unbounded boundary satisfying the small Jones’
\beta_{\infty,\partial\Omega}
coefficient and small
{\operatorname{BMO}}
norm of
\nu
conditions (
3.7
) and (
3.8
) at large scales.

We allow the constants to depend on
n
,
1<p<\infty
and the CAD constants of
\Omega
, and this will not be specified in the computations from now on in this section.

5.1.
Relation between
\nu
and the unit normal vector from
\beta_{\infty,\partial\Omega}

Given by (
3.7
), for each
x\in\partial\Omega
and
r\geq S
, we fix an
n
-plane
L_{B(x,r)}\ni x
such that

(5.1)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline hoiceofplaneLinbetaslargescales}\sup_{y\in\partial\Omega\cap B(x,r)}\frac{{\rm dist}(y,L_{B(x,r)})}{r}\leq 2\delta_{\beta},

and we denote its orthogonal unit vectors as
\pm N_{B(x,r)}
. The orientation of
N_{B(x,r)}
is fixed below in (
5.3
).

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
x\in\partial\Omega
and
r\geq S
. For all
y\in B(x,r)\cap\partial\Omega
,

(1)

|\langle x-y,N_{B(x,r)}\rangle|\leq 2\delta_{\beta}r
, and

(2)

|\langle x-y,m_{B(x,r)}\nu\rangle|\lesssim\delta_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2n}}r
.

Proof.

The second item is proved in (
4.2
). For the first item, write
B=B(x,r)
and let
N_{B}
and
L_{B}\ni x
be as in (
5.1
). Hence
{\rm dist}(y,L_{B})\leq 2\delta_{\beta}r
for all
y\in B(x,r)\cap\partial\Omega
. For each
y\in B(x,r)
let
\alpha_{y}=\angle(N_{B},y-x)
denote the angle between
N_{B}
and
y-x
. Therefore,
|{\cos\alpha_{y}}|=|{\sin(\pi/2-\alpha_{y})}|={\rm dist}(y,L_{B})/|x-y|\leq 2\delta_{\beta}r/|x-y|
, and so
|\langle x-y,N_{B}\rangle|=|x-y||{\cos\alpha_{y}}|\leq 2\delta_{\beta}r
.
∎

Under the conditions of the lemma above, as in
[
DS91
, Lemma 5.8]
, if
A
is big enough, depending only on the dimension and the ADR constant, then there exists
\{y_{j}\}_{j=0}^{n}\subset B(x,r)\cap\partial\Omega
with
y_{0}=x
and
{\rm dist}(y_{j},L_{j-1})\geq A^{-1}r
, where
L_{k}
is the
k
-plane passing through the points
y_{0},\dots,y_{k}
. In particular
|x-y_{j}|\approx_{A}r
and by
Lemma

5.1
we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\frac{x-y_{j}}{|x-y_{j}|},N_{B(x,r)}\right\rangle\right|\leq 2A\delta_{\beta},\text{ and}

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\frac{x-y_{j}}{|x-y_{j}|},m_{B(x,r)}\nu\right\rangle\right|\lesssim A\delta_{*}^{\frac{1}{2n}}.

Therefore, (recall
\delta=\max\{\delta_{\beta},\delta_{*}\}
)

(5.2)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline wonormalvectorsarealmostparallel}|\langle m_{B(x,r)}\nu,N_{B(x,r)}\rangle|\geq 1-C_{A}\max\{\delta_{\beta},\delta_{*}^{\frac{1}{2n}}\}\geq 1-C_{A}\delta^{\frac{1}{2n}},

meaning that
N_{B(x,r)}
and
\nu_{B(x,r)}
are almost parallel provided both
\delta
’s are small enough. By (
5.2
), choosing appropriately the orientation of
N_{B(x,r)}
, we assume that

(5.3)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline wonormalvectorsarealmostparallelindistancesense}|m_{B(x,r)}\nu-N_{B(x,r)}|^{2}\lesssim\delta^{\frac{1}{2n}}.

5.2.
Notation and parameters for the proof

Here we write the notation we use in the following sections.

•

S>0
: the first (large) scale where we have the smallness condition on the Jones’
\beta_{\infty,\partial\Omega}
coefficient (bound given by
\delta_{\beta}
) in (
3.7
) and the
{\operatorname{BMO}}
norm of the unit normal vector
\nu
(bound given by
\delta_{*}
) in (
3.8
).

•

T\gg 100S
: scale where we truncate the kernel on the large scales.

•

L
,
L_{B}
,
L_{j}
, etc: planes, depending on the situation.

•

N_{B}
or similar: unit normal vector in the Jones’
\beta_{\infty,\partial\Omega}
coefficient in (
5.1
).

•

M
: big parameter in the proof of
5.8a
.

•

\alpha
: the stopping condition in the construction of the Lipschitz graph, to obtain the Lipschitz graph with norm
\lesssim\alpha
.

•

We fix

\gamma\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\min\left\{1,p-1,\frac{1}{2n-1}\right\}>0.

In particular,
\gamma/(1+\gamma)<1/2n
. We will use
\tau^{\frac{1}{2n}}<\tau^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}
when
\tau\in(0,1)
repeatedly.

•

Let
{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}={\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma,\partial\Omega}
denote the uncentered Hardy-Littlewood maximal function defined in (
2.7
). Recall it is bounded from
L^{p}(\sigma)
to
L^{p}(\sigma)
with norm
C_{p}
, since
1+\gamma<p\leq\infty
and
\gamma>0
is already fixed depending on
p
.

Given
S>0
and
\delta=\max\{\delta_{\beta},\delta_{*}\}
we take

(5.4)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline hoiceofA}A=\delta^{-\theta}\text{ with }\theta=\frac{\gamma}{2(n+3)(1+\gamma)},

M=A^{1+\frac{1}{n+1}}
,
\alpha=\delta^{\frac{\gamma}{3(1+\gamma)}}
and
T=SA^{2}\gg 100S
. With this choice, we claim that, as
\delta\to 0
, there holds:

(C1)

A\to\infty
,

(C2)

A/M\to 0
,

(C3)

\delta^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}A\leq\delta^{\frac{1}{4n}(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\gamma}})}A^{2}\leq\delta^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}M^{n+1}A\to 0
,

(C4)

\delta^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}\leq\alpha^{3}\ll\alpha\to 0
,

(C5)

\alpha A^{2}\to 0
, and

(C6)

AS/T\to 0
.

Indeed, (
C1
), (
C2
), (
C4
) and (
C6
) are immediate from the choice of parameters. For (
C3
) we have

\delta^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}M^{n+1}A=\delta^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}A^{(1+\frac{1}{n+1})(n+1)+1}=\delta^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}-\theta(n+3)}=\delta^{\frac{1}{2}\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}\to 0.

For (
C5
),

\alpha A^{2}=\delta^{\frac{\gamma}{3(1+\gamma)}-2\theta}=\delta^{(\frac{1}{3}-\frac{1}{n+3})\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}\to 0.

5.3.
Reduction to a good lambda inequality

In order to estimate the
L^{p}(\sigma)
norm of
K_{l,*}=K_{l(T),*}
in (
3.9
), it suffices to prove the following good lambda inequality

(5.5)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline oodlambda1}\sigma(\{x\in\partial\Omega:K_{l,*}f(x)>101\lambda,{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\})\leq c_{\delta}\sigma(\{x\in\partial\Omega:K_{l,*}f(x)>\lambda\}),

for the parameters fixed above in (
5.4
) with
\delta
small enough, and
c_{\delta}\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
. A standard and routine computation using Fubini’s theorem (see
[
Mat95
, Theorem 1.15]
for instance), the
L^{p}
bound of the maximal operator
{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}
(see (
2.7
)) and the good lambda inequality (
5.5
) provides that there exists
\delta_{p}>0
such that if
\delta\leq\delta_{p}
then

(5.6)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:exactLpnormoflargescalesdlp}\|K_{l,*}f\|_{L^{p}(\sigma)}\lesssim_{p,n}\frac{1}{A}\|f\|_{L^{p}(\sigma)}.

By the choice of
A=\delta^{-\theta}
in (
5.4
), we obtain (
3.9
) with constants depending only on the dimension and
p
. In the other case, i.e., if
\delta>\delta_{p}
, then (
3.9
) follows directly from (
3.5a
), with constants depending also on the CAD constants of
\Omega
.

5.4.
Localization of the good lambda inequality (
5.5
)

First, let us see the classical Whitney decomposition.

Lemma 5.2

(Whitney decomposition)
.

If
U\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
is open,
U\not={\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
, then
U
can be covered as

U=\bigcup_{i\in I}Q_{i},

where
Q_{i}
,
i\in I
, are (classical) dyadic cubes in
{\mathcal{D}}({\mathbb{R}}^{n+1})
with disjoint interiors such that the following holds:

(1)

5Q_{i}\subset U
for each
i\in I
.

(2)

11Q_{i}\cap U^{c}\not=\emptyset
for each
i\in I
.

(3)

If
3Q_{i}\cap 3Q_{j}\not=\emptyset
,
i,j\in I
, then
1/(7\sqrt{n+1})\leq\ell(Q_{i})/\ell(Q_{j})\leq 7\sqrt{n+1}
.

(4)

The family
\{3Q_{i}\}_{i\in I}
has finite overlap with constant depending only on the dimension.

Proof.

The family
\mathcal{F}=\{Q_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of maximal dyadic cubes
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}({\mathbb{R}}^{n+1})
such that
5Q\subset U
satisfies the above properties by standard arguments.
∎

For
\lambda>0
, let
W_{\lambda}
be the family of dyadic cubes given by
Lemma

5.2
of the open set
\Omega_{\lambda}\coloneqq{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\setminus\{x\in\partial\Omega:K_{l,*}f(x)\leq\lambda\}
. We will refer to
W_{\lambda}
as the Whitney decomposition of
\Omega_{\lambda}
. So, we can write

V_{\lambda}\coloneqq\partial\Omega\cap\Omega_{\lambda}=\{x\in\partial\Omega:K_{l,*}f(x)>\lambda\}=\bigcup_{Q\in W_{\lambda}}Q\cap\partial\Omega.

Note that
11Q\cap(\partial\Omega\setminus V_{\lambda})\not=\emptyset
for all
Q\in W_{\lambda}
and
\{3Q\}_{Q\in W_{\lambda}}
has finite overlap.

Notation
.

We will sometimes use
Q
instead of
Q\cap\partial\Omega
when it is clear from the context.

Lemma 5.3
.

For
Q\in W_{\lambda}
, there holds

(5.7)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:largescaleslocalization}\{x\in Q\cap\partial\Omega:K_{l,*}f(x)>101\lambda,{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\}\subset\{x\in Q\cap\partial\Omega:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3Q})(x)>50\lambda\}.

We note that if
A
were small, the proof of (
5.7
) would be standard. However, its dependence on
\delta
makes the proof more involved, relying on the flatness assumptions (
3.7
) and (
3.8
).

Proof of
Lemma

5.3
.

Let
x\in Q\cap\partial\Omega
satisfy
K_{l,*}f(x)>101\lambda
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda
, and let
z\in 11Q\cap(\partial\Omega\setminus V_{\lambda})\not=\emptyset
so that
K_{l,*}f(z)\leq\lambda
. We have
x,z\in 11Q\subset B(z,11(n+1)^{1/2}\ell(Q))\eqqcolon B
, and so
r(B)\approx\ell(Q)
.

The lemma will follow from

(5.8a)

\displaystyle K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{B^{c}})(x)\leq 41\lambda,\text{ and}

(5.8b)

\displaystyle K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{B\setminus 3Q})(x)\leq 10\lambda,

because

K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3Q})(x)\geq K_{l,*}f(x)-K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{B^{c}})(x)-K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{B\setminus 3Q})(x)\geq 50\lambda.

We first prove (
5.8a
). For
M>0
big enough (auxiliary parameter only used in this proof, already defined depending on
A
, see (
C2
) and (
C3
)) write

\displaystyle K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{B^{c}})(x)

\displaystyle=K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{B^{c}})(z)+K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{B^{c}})(x)-K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{B^{c}})(z)

\displaystyle\leq K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{B^{c}})(z)+\left|K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{MQ\setminus B})(x)-K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{MQ\setminus B})(z)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{(MQ)^{c}})(x)-K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{(MQ)^{c}})(z)\right|

\displaystyle\eqqcolon\,\boxed{i}+\boxed{ii}+\boxed{iii}.

Since
B
is centered at
z
and by the choice of
z
we have

\boxed{i}\leq K_{l,*}f(z)\leq\lambda.

Let us bound the term
\boxed{ii}
. We simply have

\boxed{ii}\lesssim\frac{1}{r_{B}^{n+1}}\left(\int_{MQ\setminus B}|\langle\nu(y),y-x\rangle||f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)+\int_{MQ\setminus B}|\langle\nu(y),y-z\rangle||f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)\right).

For
\xi=x
or
\xi=z
, we have

\int_{MQ\setminus B}|\langle\nu(y),y-\xi\rangle||f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)\leq\int_{B(\xi,\sqrt{n+1}M\ell(Q))}|\langle\nu(y),y-\xi\rangle||f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)\eqqcolon\boxed{ii_{\xi}}.

Set
B_{\xi}\coloneqq B(\xi,\sqrt{n+1}M\ell(Q))
. By the triangle inequality (after adding
\pm m_{B_{\xi}}\nu
), Hölder’s inequality, (
4.2
) and
{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda
we have

\displaystyle\boxed{ii_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{\xi}}|\langle\nu(y)-m_{B_{\xi}}\nu,y-\xi\rangle||f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)+\int_{B_{\xi}}|\langle m_{B_{\xi}}\nu,y-\xi\rangle||f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{n+1}\ell(Q)^{n+1}\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B_{\xi}}|\nu(y)-m_{B_{\xi}}\nu|^{\frac{1+\gamma}{\gamma}}\,d\sigma(y)\right)^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B_{\xi}}|f(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d\sigma(y)\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{\xi}}|\langle m_{B_{\xi}}\nu,y-\xi\rangle||f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{{(\ref{coro:pointwise estimate vector dot product})}}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\lesssim}M^{n+1}\ell(Q)^{n+1}\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)+M^{n+1}\ell(Q)^{n+1}\delta_{*}^{\frac{1}{2n}}{\mathcal{M}}_{1}f(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{n+1}\ell(Q)^{n+1}\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}A\lambda.

Note that even though we are studying the term
\boxed{ii_{\xi}}
, in any case we controlled
\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B_{\xi}}|f|\,d\sigma
and
(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B_{\xi}}|f|^{1+\gamma}\,d\sigma)^{1/(1+\gamma)}
by
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}f(x)
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)
respectively, and so we finally used that
{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda
. Hence,

\boxed{ii}\lesssim\frac{1}{r_{B}^{n+1}}M^{n+1}\ell(Q)^{n+1}\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}A\lambda\approx M^{n+1}\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}A\lambda.

Let us bound
\boxed{iii}
. For
\varepsilon\geq T
, we have

|K_{\varepsilon}(f\mathbf{1}_{(MQ)^{c}})(x)-K_{\varepsilon}(f\mathbf{1}_{(MQ)^{c}})(z)|\leq\boxed{A}+\boxed{B}+\boxed{C},

where

\displaystyle\boxed{A}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left|\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}|y-x|>\varepsilon\\
|y-z|>\varepsilon\end{subarray}}\left\langle\nu(y)f(y)\mathbf{1}_{(MQ)^{c}}(y),\frac{y-x}{|x-y|^{n+1}}-\frac{y-z}{|z-y|^{n+1}}\right\rangle\,d\sigma(y)\right|,

\displaystyle\boxed{B}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left|\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}|y-x|>\varepsilon\\
|y-z|\leq\varepsilon\end{subarray}}\frac{\langle\nu(y),y-x\rangle}{|x-y|^{n+1}}f(y)\mathbf{1}_{(MQ)^{c}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)\right|,\text{ and}

\displaystyle\boxed{C}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left|\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}|y-z|>\varepsilon\\
|y-x|\leq\varepsilon\end{subarray}}\frac{\langle\nu(y),y-z\rangle}{|z-y|^{n+1}}f(y)\mathbf{1}_{(MQ)^{c}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)\right|.

We first study the term
\boxed{A}
. Using the cancellation property of the Calderón-Zygmund kernel first (this is just the mean value theorem), and
B(x,M\ell(Q)/2)\subset MQ
and
|x-z|\lesssim\ell(Q)
second, we have

\boxed{A}\lesssim\int_{y\not\in MQ}|f(y)|\frac{|x-z|}{|x-y|^{n+1}}\,d\sigma(y)\lesssim\ell(Q)\int_{y\not\in B(x,\frac{M}{2}\ell(Q))}\frac{|f(y)|}{|x-y|^{n+1}}\,d\sigma(y).

Note that the latter integral does not depend on
z
. Breaking the latter integral into annuli and by the AD regularity of
\sigma
, using standard estimates we get

\boxed{A}\lesssim\frac{1}{M}{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq\frac{A}{M}\lambda.

We now turn to the terms
\boxed{B}
and
\boxed{C}
. First we note that we can assume that
\varepsilon\geq\frac{M-10}{2}\ell(Q)\geq\frac{M}{4}\ell(Q)
, otherwise
B(z,\varepsilon)\subset MQ
and so
\boxed{B}=0
. For
\varepsilon>\frac{M}{4}\ell(Q)
we have
B(z,\varepsilon)\subset B(x,2\varepsilon)
, and we get

\displaystyle\boxed{B}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{B(x,2\varepsilon)\setminus\overline{B(x,\varepsilon)}}\frac{|\langle\nu(y),y-x\rangle|}{|x-y|^{n+1}}|f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x,2\varepsilon)}|\langle\nu(y),y-x\rangle||f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x,2\varepsilon)}|\langle\nu(y)-m_{B(x,2\varepsilon)}\nu,y-x\rangle||f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x,2\varepsilon)}|\langle m_{B(x,2\varepsilon)}\nu,y-x\rangle||f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x,2\varepsilon)}|\nu(y)-m_{B(x,2\varepsilon)}\nu|^{\frac{1+\gamma}{\gamma}}\,d\sigma(y)\right)^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x,2\varepsilon)}|f(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d\sigma(y)\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\left(\sup_{y\in\partial\Omega\cap B(x,2\varepsilon)}\frac{|\langle x-y,m_{B(x,2\varepsilon)}\nu\rangle|}{\varepsilon}\right)\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x,2\varepsilon)}|f(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d\sigma(y)\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathclap{\text{{(\ref{coro:pointwise estimate vector dot product})}}}}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle(\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}+\delta_{*}^{\frac{1}{2n}}){\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\lesssim\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}A\lambda.

By symmetry, but using that
B(z,\varepsilon)\subset B(x,2\varepsilon)
(recall we can assume
\varepsilon>\frac{M}{4}\ell(Q)
) before applying the bound of the maximal operator
{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}
, we have the same bound for
\boxed{C}
, that is,
\boxed{C}\lesssim\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}A\lambda
. Therefore, the term
\boxed{iii}
is controlled by

\boxed{iii}\leq\boxed{A}+\boxed{B}+\boxed{C}\lesssim\left(\frac{A}{M}+\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}A\right)\lambda

From the bound of
\boxed{i}
,
\boxed{ii}
and
\boxed{iii}
, and conditions (
C2
) and (
C3
) we conclude

K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{B^{c}})(x)\leq\lambda+C\left(M^{n+1}\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}A+\frac{A}{M}+\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}A\right)\lambda\leq 41\lambda,

as claimed in (
5.8a
).

It remains to prove (
5.8b
). For
\varepsilon\geq T
, we may assume
\{y:|y-x|>\varepsilon\}\cap B\not=\emptyset
, otherwise
K_{\varepsilon}(f\mathbf{1}_{B\setminus 3Q})(x)=0
. Thus,
r(B)\gtrsim\varepsilon\geq T
, allowing us to apply (
4.2
). Using that
\ell(Q)\approx r(B)
and that
|y-x|\gtrsim\ell(Q)
if
y\not\in 3Q
, and arguing in the last inequality as in the bound for
\boxed{ii_{\xi}}
, we get

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon}(f\mathbf{1}_{B\setminus 3Q})(x)

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{|y-x|>\varepsilon}\frac{\langle\nu(y),y-x\rangle}{|x-y|^{n+1}}f(y)\mathbf{1}_{B\setminus 3Q}(y)\,d\sigma(y)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\ell(Q)}\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B}|\langle\nu(y)-m_{B}\nu,y-x\rangle||f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)+\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B}|\langle m_{B}\nu,y-x\rangle||f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{*}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\gamma}}A\lambda,

uniformly in
\varepsilon\geq T
. Then (
5.8b
) follows by condition (
C3
).
∎

By (
5.7
), in order to prove (
5.5
) we claim that it suffices to see

(5.9)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline oodlambda2}\sigma(\{x\in Q:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3Q})(x)>50\lambda,{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\})\leq c_{\delta}\sigma(3Q),

for all
Q\in W_{\lambda}
. Indeed,

\sigma(\{x\in\partial\Omega:K_{l,*}f(x)>101\lambda,{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\})\\
\begin{aligned} &\leq\sum_{Q\in W_{\lambda}}\sigma(\{x\in Q:K_{l,*}f(x)>101\lambda,{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\})\\
\overset{\text{{(\ref{eq:large scales localization})}}}{}&{\leq}\sum_{Q\in W_{\lambda}}\sigma(\{x\in Q:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3Q})(x)>50\lambda,{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\})\\
\overset{\text{{(\ref{good lambda 2})}}}{}&{\leq}c_{\delta}\sum_{Q\in W_{\lambda}}\sigma(3Q)\lesssim c_{\delta}\sigma(\Omega_{\lambda})=c_{\delta}\sigma(V_{\lambda}),\end{aligned}

where in the last step we used the finite overlap of the family
\{3Q\}_{Q\in W_{\lambda}}
.

Remark 5.4
.

From now on we can assume without loss of generality that
\ell(Q)\geq\frac{T}{10(n+1)^{1/2}}
, otherwise, if
x\in Q
then
\{y\in 3Q:|y-x|>T\}=\emptyset
and hence

K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3Q})(x)=\sup_{\varepsilon\geq T}\left|\int_{y\in 3Q\setminus\overline{B(x,\varepsilon)}}\frac{\langle\nu(y),y-x\rangle}{|x-y|^{n+1}}f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3Q}(y)\,d\sigma(y)\right|=0.

5.5.
Construction of a Lipschitz graph

During this section, let
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
be an ADR domain with unbounded boundary, and let
{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma}
denote its dyadic lattice in
Lemma

2.12
. For
t>1
and
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma}
we set

(5.10)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline ilatedboundarycube}tQ\coloneqq\{x\in\partial\Omega:{\rm dist}(x,Q)\leq(t-1){\rm diam}\,Q\}.

Notation

(Dependence of parameters)
.

Fixed
m>1
, we choose
k_{0}=k_{0}(m)\gg m
and
k=k(k_{0})\gg k_{0}
. We consider
\varepsilon,\alpha\ll 1
such that
\varepsilon\ll\alpha
and
\varepsilon\ll 1/k_{0}
. Throughout this section, we allow the constant
C
to depend on the ADR constant, but not on the parameters mentioned above.

Following
[
DS91
, Section 8]
, in this section we construct a Lipschitz approximating graph at large scales (see
Proposition

5.9
), which only uses the following flatness assumption at large scales: We assume that for each cube
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma}
with
{\rm diam}\,Q\geq S
, there exists an
n
-plane
L_{Q}
such that

(5.11)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline ineapproxcube}{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})\leq\varepsilon{\rm diam}\,Q\text{ for all }x\in kQ.

We will see in
Remark

5.16
in
Section

5.6
below that this is guaranteed under the assumption (
3.7
).

We recall that the
n
-plane in (
5.11
) is almost unique in the following sense.

Lemma 5.5

(See
[
DS91
, Lemma 5.13]
)
.

Let
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma}
and assume that
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are two
n
-planes such that
{\rm dist}(x,L_{i})\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}{\rm diam}\,Q
for all
x\in Q
,
i=1,2
. Then
\angle(L_{1},L_{2})\leq C\varepsilon^{\prime}
.

Let us fix
R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma,j_{0}}
with
j_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
so that
2^{-j_{0}}\geq S
; in particular
{\rm diam}\,Q\geq S
for all
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma,j_{0}}
. Fixed
m>1
, we define the “
m
-neighborhood cubes” of
R
as the collection of cubes in
{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma,j_{0}}
touching
mR
. That is,

U_{m}(R)\coloneqq\{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma,j_{0}}:Q\cap mR\not=\emptyset\}.

So, for every
Q\in U_{m}(R)
there holds
{\rm dist}(Q,R)\leq(m-1){\rm diam}\,R
. For any two cubes
Q_{1},Q_{2}\in U_{m}(R)
, if
x\in Q_{1}
then

{\rm dist}(x,Q_{2})\leq{\rm diam}\,Q_{1}+{\rm dist}(Q_{1},R)+{\rm diam}\,R+{\rm dist}(R,Q_{2})\leq C_{\mathcal{D}}2m{\rm diam}\,Q_{2},

and therefore

Q_{1}\subset(C_{\mathcal{D}}2m+1)Q_{2}.

Fix a constant
k\gg m
(at least
k\geq C_{\mathcal{D}}2m+1
), so that the inclusion above implies

Q_{1}\subset kQ_{2}\text{ for any }Q_{1},Q_{2}\in U_{m}(R).

As a consequence of this, (
5.11
) and
Lemma

5.5
, we have

(5.12)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:anglebetweencubesinUmR}\angle(L_{Q_{1}},L_{Q_{2}})\lesssim\varepsilon\text{ for any }Q_{1},Q_{2}\in U_{m}(R).

Definition 5.6
.

We say that
Q\in\bigcup_{j\geq j_{0}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma,j}
is a stopping cube with respect to
R
, and we write
Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}(R)
, if
Q\subset\bigcup_{Q^{\prime}\in U_{m}(R)}Q^{\prime}
and
Q
is maximal such that either

(1)

{\rm diam}\,Q<S
, or

(2)

{\rm diam}\,Q\geq S
and
\angle(L_{R},L_{Q})>\alpha
, recall
L_{R}
and
L_{Q}
as in (
5.11
).

Definition 5.7
.

We define the set
{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)
as the collection of cubes
P\in\bigcup_{j\geq j_{0}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma,j}
such that

(1)

P\subset\bigcup_{Q^{\prime}\in U_{m}(R)}Q^{\prime}
, and

(2)

P\not\subset Q
for any
Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}(R)
.

In particular, every
Q\in{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)
satisfies
{\rm diam}\,Q\geq S
and
\angle(L_{R},L_{Q})\leq\alpha
.

Remark 5.8
.

In contrast to the construction in
[
DS91
, Section 8]
, where only dyadic subcubes of
R
are considered, here we take dyadic subcubes of
U_{m}(R)
in the construction of
{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)
. In our situation, since
\varepsilon\ll\alpha
and
{\rm diam}\,Q\geq S
for all
Q\in U_{m}(R)
, we note that (
5.12
) ensures that
Q\not\in{\mathsf{Stop}}(R)
for all
Q\in U_{m}(R)
. Consequently, given a cube in
{\mathsf{Stop}}(R)
, its parent is in
{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)
.

Consider

d(x)=d_{R}(x)\coloneqq\inf_{Q\in{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)}\{{\rm dist}(x,Q)+{\rm diam}\,Q\},\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}.

Since
\#{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)<\infty
, the infimum is in fact a minimum. Note that
d(\cdot)\geq S
and is
1
-Lipschitz, as it is the infimum of
1
-Lipschitz functions. Clearly, if
x\in Q\in{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)
then
d(x)\leq{\rm diam}\,Q
.

In this section, we prove the following result, which is the analog of
[
DS91
, Proposition 8.2]
. In this lemma and throughout this section,
L_{R}^{\perp}
denotes the orthogonal line to the
n
-plane
L_{R}
(see (
5.11
)),
\Pi=\Pi_{R}
denotes the orthogonal projection onto
L_{R}
, and
\Pi^{\perp}=\Pi^{\perp}_{R}
denotes the orthogonal projection onto
L_{R}^{\perp}
.

Proposition 5.9
.

For
R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma,j_{0}}
with
j_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
so that
2^{-j_{0}}\geq S
(in particular
{\rm diam}\,R\geq S
), there exists
A:L_{R}\to L_{R}^{\perp}
Lipschitz graph with norm
\leq C\alpha
such that

(5.13)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:constructionLipgraph}{\rm dist}\left(x,\left(\Pi_{R}(x),A(\Pi_{R}(x))\right)\right)\leq C\varepsilon d_{R}(x)\text{ for all }x\in k_{0}R.

We define in
L_{R}
the
1
-Lipschitz function

(5.14)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline efofD}D(p)=D_{R}(p)\coloneqq\inf_{x\in\Pi^{-1}(p)}d_{R}(x),\quad p\in L_{R},

which can be rewritten as

D(p)=\inf_{x\in\Pi^{-1}(p)}\inf_{Q\in{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)}\{{\rm dist}(x,Q)+{\rm diam}\,Q\}=\inf_{Q\in{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)}\{{\rm dist}(p,\Pi_{R}(Q))+{\rm diam}\,Q\}.

As
d\geq S
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
, in particular
D\geq S
in
L_{R}
. As before, since
\#{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)<\infty
, the latter infimum is in fact a minimum.

Arguing as in the proof of
[
DS91
, Lemma 8.4]
, we obtain the following, as the proof relies only on the fact that every
Q\in{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)
satisfies
\angle(L_{R},L_{Q})\leq\alpha
.

Lemma 5.10

(
[
DS91
, Lemma 8.4]
)
.

If
x,y\in 10k_{0}R
satisfy
|x-y|\geq 10^{-3}\min\{d(x),d(y)\}
, then

|\Pi^{\perp}(x)-\Pi^{\perp}(y)|\leq 2\alpha|\Pi(x)-\Pi(y)|.

Now we decompose
L_{R}
in classical dyadic cubes using the function
D_{R}
. First, we shall identify
L_{R}
with
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, and in particular we equip
L_{R}
with classical dyadic cubes
{\mathcal{D}}_{L_{R}}\coloneqq{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}
. For each
x\in L_{R}
(recall
D_{R}\geq S>0
in
L_{R}
), let
R_{x}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{L_{R}}
be the largest dyadic cube containing
x
and satisfying

(5.15)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline hoiceofdyadicdecompositionofhyperplane}{\rm diam}\,R_{x}\leq 20^{-1}\inf_{u\in R_{x}}D(u).

We relabel them without repetition as
\{R_{i}\}_{i\in I}
. Thus, the family
\{R_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is pairwise disjoint and covers
L_{R}
. (As in
[
DS91
, Section 8]
, we use the convention that dyadic cubes are closed but are called disjoint if their interiors are disjoint.)

Recall that the definition of
tQ
is slightly different depending on whether
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma}
or
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{L_{R}}={\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}
, see (
5.10
) and
Section

2.1
respectively.

From the definition of the family
\{R_{i}\}_{i\in I}
using (
5.15
) and that
D
is
1
-Lipschitz in
L_{R}
, by the same proof of
[
DS91
, Lemma 8.7]
we have:

Lemma 5.11

(
[
DS91
, Lemma 8.7]
)
.

For all
y\in 10R_{i}
,
i\in I
,

(5.16)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline omparabilityDanddiamRi}10{\rm diam}\,R_{i}\leq D(y)\leq 60{\rm diam}\,R_{i},

and in particular, if
10R_{i}\cap 10R_{j}\not=\emptyset
,
i,j\in I
, then

(5.17)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline losedyadiccubeshavecomparablediameter}6^{-1}{\rm diam}\,R_{i}\leq{\rm diam}\,R_{j}\leq 6{\rm diam}\,R_{i}.

Let
U_{0}\coloneqq L_{R}\cap B(\Pi(x_{R}),2k_{0}{\rm diam}\,R)
, where
x_{R}
is any fixed point in
R
, and
I_{0}\coloneqq\{i\in I:R_{i}\cap U_{0}\not=\emptyset\}
. We fix
k_{0}\gg m
so that
\bigcup_{Q^{\prime}\in U_{m}(R)}\Pi(Q^{\prime})\subset U_{0}
. For each
i\in I_{0}
, let
Q(i)\in{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)
be such that
5
5

5

The correspondence
I_{0}\ni i\mapsto Q(i)
may not be injective.

(5.18a)

\displaystyle C^{-1}\frac{1}{k_{0}}{\rm diam}\,R_{i}\leq{\rm diam}\,Q(i)\leq C{\rm diam}\,R_{i},\text{ and}

(5.18b)

\displaystyle{\rm dist}(\Pi(Q(i)),R_{i})\leq C{\rm diam}\,R_{i}.

Such cubes
Q(i)
exist, because if
p\in R_{i}
, then there exists
Q\in{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)
such that

{\rm dist}(p,\Pi(Q))+{\rm diam}\,Q\leq 2D(p)\overset{\text{{(\ref{comparability D and diamRi})}}}{\leq}120{\rm diam}\,R_{i},

and we can take
Q(i)
to be the maximal cube in
{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)
with
Q\subset Q(i)
and
{\rm diam}\,Q(i)\leq 120{\rm diam}\,R_{i}
.

Definition 5.12
.

For
i\in I_{0}
, let
A_{i}:L_{R}\to L_{R}^{\perp}
denote the affine function whose graph is the
n
-plane
L_{Q(i)}
. By the stopping condition, the Lipschitz norm is
\leq 2\alpha
.

We consider a partition of unity for
V=\bigcup_{i\in I_{0}}2R_{i}
. That is, a family
\{\phi_{i}\}_{i\in I_{0}}
satisfying for all
i\in I_{0}
that
\phi_{i}\geq 0
,
\phi_{i}\in C^{1}_{c}(3R_{i})
(hence
\{\operatorname{supp}\phi_{i}\}_{i\in I_{0}}
has finite overlap, by
5.17
) and

|\nabla\phi_{i}|\lesssim\frac{1}{{\rm diam}\,R_{i}}.

Finally we define
A
on
V
by

A(p)\coloneqq\sum_{i\in I_{0}}\phi_{i}(p)A_{i}(p)\text{ for }p\in V.

By the same proof in
[
DS91
, Lemma 8.17]
we have:

Lemma 5.13

(
[
DS91
, Lemma 8.17]
)
.

If
10R_{i}\cap 10R_{j}\not=\emptyset
, then
{\rm dist}(Q(i),Q(j))\leq C{\rm diam}\,R_{j}
and

(5.19)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline amepointbuttwoaffinemaps}|A_{i}(q)-A_{j}(q)|\leq C\varepsilon{\rm diam}\,R_{j}\text{ for all }q\in 100R_{j}.

Using the previous lemma, the same proof in
[
DS91
, Section 8, p. 46]
applies to see that the restriction of
A
to
2R_{j}
,
j\in I_{0}
, is
3\alpha
-Lipschitz.

Lemma 5.14

(
[
DS91
, (8.19)]
)
.

If
p,q\in 2R_{j}
,
j\in I_{0}
, then

|A(p)-A(q)|\leq 3\alpha|p-q|.

We now aim to show that
A
is
C\alpha
-Lipschitz in
U_{0}
. We remark that this is the analog of
[
DS91
, (8.20)]
, with the difference that in our case, we have
D(\cdot)\geq S>0
in
L_{R}
. For the sake of completeness, we provide the full details of the proof.

Lemma 5.15
.

If
p,q\in U_{0}
, then
|A(p)-A(q)|\leq C\alpha|p-q|
.

Proof.

Since
D\geq S>0
, all points in
U_{0}
belong to some (unique)
R_{k}
, where
k\in I_{0}
. Let
i,j\in I_{0}
be such that
p\in R_{i}
and
q\in R_{j}
. If
p,q\in 2R_{i}
or
p,q\in 2R_{j}
, then
|A(p)-A(q)|\leq 3\alpha|p-q|
by
Lemma

5.14
, and we are done. Hence, from now on, we may assume that
q\not\in 2R_{i}
and
p\not\in 2R_{j}
. In particular, this implies

(5.20)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline asepointsarefarforeachdyadiccube}|p-q|\geq\max\{{\rm diam}\,R_{i},{\rm diam}\,R_{j}\}.

Let
y\in Q(i)
and
z\in Q(j)
be such that
|y-z|=\sup_{a\in Q(i),b\in Q(j)}|a-b|
, which in particular implies

(5.21)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline hoiceofyandzconsequence}|y-z|\geq\frac{1}{2}\min\{{\rm diam}\,Q(i),{\rm diam}\,Q(j)\}.

We have

\displaystyle|A(p)-A(q)|\leq

\displaystyle|A(p)-A_{i}(p)|+|A_{i}(p)-A_{i}(\Pi(y))|+|A_{i}(\Pi(y))-\Pi^{\perp}(y)|

\displaystyle+|\Pi^{\perp}(y)-\Pi^{\perp}(z)|

\displaystyle+|A(q)-A_{j}(q)|+|A_{j}(q)-A_{j}(\Pi(z))|+|A_{j}(\Pi(z))-\Pi^{\perp}(z)|.

Let us see that all the terms are bounded by
\lesssim\alpha
. We bound the first four terms, and the last three follow by symmetry.

Term
|A(p)-A_{i}(p)|
: Using the partition of unity in the second equality, and by (
5.19
) in
Lemma

5.13
(since
p\in R_{i}
, the fact that
\phi_{j}(p)\not=0
implies that
\operatorname{supp}\phi_{j}\cap R_{i}\not=\emptyset
, and in particular
3R_{j}\cap R_{i}\not=\emptyset
) and since the last sum runs over the index
j\in I_{0}
around
R_{i}
(in particular a finite number of candidates), we have

(5.22)

\displaystyle|A(p)-A_{i}(p)|

\displaystyle=\left|\left(\sum_{j\in I_{0}}\phi_{j}(p)A_{j}(p)\right)-A_{i}(p)\right|=\left|\sum_{j\in I_{0}}\phi_{j}(p)(A_{j}(p)-A_{i}(p))\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{{(\ref{same point but two affine maps})}}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\leq}C\varepsilon{\rm diam}\,R_{i}\overset{\text{{(\ref{case points are far for each dyadic cube})}}}{\leq}C\varepsilon|p-q|\leq\alpha|p-q|.

Term
|A_{i}(p)-A_{i}(\Pi(y))|
: First,
|A_{i}(p)-A_{i}(\Pi(y))|\leq 2\alpha|p-\Pi(y)|
since
A_{i}
is
2\alpha
-Lipschitz, and second
|p-\Pi(y)|\leq{\rm diam}\,R_{i}+{\rm dist}(R_{i},\Pi(Q(i)))+{\rm diam}\,Q(i)
, which by the choice of
Q(i)
in (
5.18a
) and (
5.18b
) the last two terms are controlled by
\lesssim{\rm diam}\,R_{i}
. We conclude this term by using (
5.20
).

Term
|A_{i}(\Pi(y))-\Pi^{\perp}(y)|
: Since
\angle(L_{Q(i)},L_{R})\leq\alpha
is small and by (
5.11
), we have
|A_{i}(\Pi(y))-\Pi^{\perp}(y)|\leq 2{\rm dist}(y,L_{Q(i)})\leq 2\varepsilon{\rm diam}\,Q(i)
. By the choice of
Q(i)
in (
5.18a
) this is controlled by
\leq C\varepsilon{\rm diam}\,R_{i}
, and by (
5.20
) the last term is controlled by
\leq C\varepsilon|p-q|\leq\alpha|p-q|
.

Term
|\Pi^{\perp}(y)-\Pi^{\perp}(z)|
: First,
y\in Q(i)
implies that
d(y)\leq{\rm diam}\,Q(i)
, and
z\in Q(j)
implies
d(z)\leq{\rm diam}\,Q(j)
. Thus, from (
5.21
) and this we have
|y-z|\geq\frac{1}{2}\min\{{\rm diam}\,Q(i),{\rm diam}\,Q(j)\}\geq\frac{1}{2}\min\{d(y),d(z)\}
. Using
Lemma

5.10
in the first inequality we get

|\Pi^{\perp}(y)-\Pi^{\perp}(z)|\leq 2\alpha|\Pi(y)-\Pi(z)|\leq 2\alpha\left(|\Pi(y)-p|+|p-q|+|q-\Pi(z)|\right).

As in the bound of the second term (that is,
|A_{i}(p)-A_{i}(\Pi(y))|
), the first term inside the brackets is bounded by
\leq C{\rm diam}\,R_{i}
. By symmetry, the third term is controlled by
\leq C{\rm diam}\,R_{j}
. This term follows by (
5.20
) as well.
∎

Now we can use the Whitney extension theorem to extend
A
from
U_{0}
to a
C\alpha
-Lipschitz function on all
L_{R}
. This gives the existence of the
C\alpha
-Lipschitz graph in
Proposition

5.9
. It remains to see now that this
C\alpha
-Lipschitz graph approximates
k_{0}R
in the (
5.13
) sense.

Proof of (
5.13
).

Let
x\in k_{0}R
and set
p=\Pi(x)
. Recall
D(p)\geq S>0
, and let
R_{i}
,
i\in I_{0}
, so that
p\in R_{i}
. We first break

{\rm dist}(x,(\Pi(x),A(\Pi(x))))\leq|\Pi^{\perp}(x)-A_{i}(\Pi(x))|+|A(\Pi(x))-A_{i}(\Pi(x))|.

Applying
[
DS91
, Lemma 8.21]
with
r=D(p)
and
Q=Q(i)
, we get that
x\in C_{k_{0}}Q(i)
for some
C_{k_{0}}
depending on
k_{0}
, so taking
k\gg C_{k_{0}}
we have
x\in kQ(i)
. Using that
\angle(L_{R},L_{Q(i)})\leq\alpha
is small, the flatness condition in (
5.11
) and the choice of
Q(i)
in (
5.18a
) respectively, we have

|\Pi^{\perp}(x)-A_{i}(\Pi(x))|\leq 2{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q(i)})\leq 2\varepsilon{\rm diam}\,Q(i)\lesssim\varepsilon{\rm diam}\,R_{i}.

The term
|A(\Pi(x))-A_{i}(\Pi(x))|
is simply the first term in the proof of
Lemma

5.15
, whence we obtain directly from (
5.22
) that

|A(\Pi(x))-A_{i}(\Pi(x))|\leq C\varepsilon{\rm diam}\,R_{i}.

All in all,
{\rm dist}(x,(\Pi(x),A(\Pi(x))))\leq C\varepsilon{\rm diam}\,R_{i}
. From the definition of the family
\{R_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in (
5.15
), we have
{\rm diam}\,R_{i}\leq 20^{-1}D(p)
. Just from the definition
D
in (
5.14
),
D(p)\leq d(x)
. This concludes the proof of (
5.13
).
∎

5.6.
Proof of the localized good lambda inequality (
5.9
)

Let
\Omega
be as in the statement of
Theorem

3.5
. To apply the notation of the section of the Lipschitz graph construction, for
\lambda>0
we write
R_{W}\in W_{\lambda}
for the cube from the Whitney covering of
\Omega_{\lambda}
(the letters
Q,R
are reserved for boundary dyadic cubes
{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma}
), and we rewrite the good lambda inequality (
5.9
) as

(5.23)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline oodlambda3}\sigma(\{x\in R_{W}:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}})(x)>50\lambda,{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\})\leq c_{\delta}\sigma(3R_{W}).

We will use without mention that we assume that there exists
x_{0}\in R_{W}
with
{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x_{0})\leq A\lambda
, otherwise the left-hand side in (
5.23
) is zero and we are done. Recall also from
Remark

5.4
that we have
\ell(R_{W})\gtrsim T\gg S
.

The thin boundary condition (
4
) in
Lemma

2.12
implies, for each
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma}
, the existence of a point
c_{Q}\in Q
, called the center of
Q
, such that
{\rm dist}(c_{Q},\partial\Omega\setminus Q)\gtrsim_{\text{ADR},n}{\rm diam}\,Q
, see
[
DS93
, Lemma 3.5 of Part I]
. We denote

(5.24)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline allinsideoutsidethecube}B(Q)\coloneqq B(c_{Q},c_{1}{\rm diam}\,Q),\quad B_{Q}\coloneqq B(c_{Q},{\rm diam}\,Q)\supset Q,

where
c_{1}\in(0,1]
is chosen so that
B(Q^{\prime})\cap\partial\Omega\subset Q^{\prime}
for all
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma}
, satisfying also that
B(Q_{1})\cap B(Q_{2})=\emptyset
for all disjoint
Q_{1},Q_{2}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma}
. Indeed, assuming without loss of generality that
{\rm diam}\,Q_{2}\leq{\rm diam}\,Q_{1}
, if
B(Q_{1})\cap B(Q_{2})\not=\emptyset
, then

{\rm diam}\,Q_{1}\lesssim_{\text{ADR},n}{\rm dist}(c_{Q_{1}},\partial\Omega\setminus Q_{1})\leq|c_{Q_{1}}-c_{Q_{2}}|\leq c_{1}({\rm diam}\,Q_{1}+{\rm diam}\,Q_{2})\leq 2c_{1}{\rm diam}\,Q_{1},

which is not possible if
c_{1}
is chosen to be sufficiently small, depending only on the ADR character of
\partial\Omega
.

We begin the proof of (
5.23
). Given
R_{W}\in W_{\lambda}
, let
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
be so that
\ell(R_{W})=2^{-j}
(recall
R_{W}\in{\mathcal{D}}({\mathbb{R}}^{n+1})
), fix any
x\in R_{W}\cap\partial\Omega
(if such point does not exist then the left-hand side of (
5.23
) is zero), and let
R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma,j}
be the cube with
x\in R
. We fix
m=3
so that
\partial\Omega\cap 3R_{W}\subset mR
. Indeed, since
{\rm diam}\,R\geq 2^{-j}
, for all
y\in R_{W}
we have
|y-x|\leq 2{\rm diam}\,R_{W}=2\cdot 2^{-j}\leq 2{\rm diam}\,R
, thus
{\rm dist}(y,R)\leq|y-x|\leq 2{\rm diam}\,R
, see (
5.10
).

For every
Q\in U_{m}(R)
and every
x\in Q
,

|x-c_{R}|\leq{\rm diam}\,Q+(m-1){\rm diam}\,R+{\rm diam}\,R\leq C_{\mathcal{D}}(m+1){\rm diam}\,R,

where
C_{\mathcal{D}}
is the constant in (
3
) in the boundary dyadic lattice
Lemma

2.12
. Also, if
x\in B_{Q}\supset B(Q)
(not necessarily in
Q
), then

|x-c_{R}|\leq|x-c_{Q}|+|c_{Q}-c_{R}|\leq{\rm diam}\,Q+C_{\mathcal{D}}(m+1){\rm diam}\,R\leq C_{\mathcal{D}}(m+2){\rm diam}\,R.

This implies that
\bigcup_{Q\in U_{m}(R)}B_{Q}\supset\bigcup_{Q\in U_{m}(R)}B(Q)
and
\bigcup_{Q\in U_{m}(R)}Q
are subsets of

(5.25)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline efofballcontainingU_{m}R}\mathfrak{B}_{R}\coloneqq C_{\mathcal{D}}(m+2)B_{R},

with
B_{R}
as in (
5.24
).

Since
\sigma
is ADR, we will use that

\sigma(3R_{W})\approx\sigma(R)\approx_{m}\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{R})\approx_{m}\sigma\left(\bigcup_{Q\in U_{m}(R)}Q\right).

Remark 5.16
.

Under the choice in (
5.1
), the estimate (
5.11
) holds with
\varepsilon=2k\delta_{\beta}
and
L_{Q}=L_{kB_{Q}}
. Indeed, as
kQ\subset kB_{Q}
we have

\sup_{x\in kQ}{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})\leq\sup_{x\in\partial\Omega\cap kB_{Q}}{\rm dist}(y,L_{Q})\overset{\text{{(\ref{choice of plane L in betas large scales})}}}{\leq}2\delta_{\beta}k{\rm diam}\,Q.

Recall in
Section

5.5
we needed
\varepsilon=2k\delta_{\beta}\ll\alpha
, which is granted by (
C4
).

We define the following subfamilies of
{\mathsf{Stop}}(R)
(see
Definition

5.6
) of big and small angle respectively as

\displaystyle{\mathsf{BA}}(R)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}(R):\angle(L_{R},L_{Q})>\alpha\},\text{ and}

\displaystyle{\mathsf{SA}}(R)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}(R):\angle(L_{R},L_{Q})\leq\alpha\},

so that
{\mathsf{Stop}}(R)={\mathsf{BA}}(R)\cup{\mathsf{SA}}(R)
and the union is disjoint. By the stopping conditions in
Definition

5.6
in the construction of the Lipschitz graph, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{BA}}(R)

\displaystyle\supset\{Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}(R):{\rm diam}\,Q\geq S\},\text{ and}

\displaystyle{\mathsf{SA}}(R)

\displaystyle\subset\{Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}(R):{\rm diam}\,Q<S\}.

Remark 5.17
.

If
Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)
, then
{\rm diam}\,Q<S
but its parent
\widehat{Q}\in{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)
satisfies
{\rm diam}\,\widehat{Q}\geq S
as
\widehat{Q}\not\in{\mathsf{Stop}}(R)
, and therefore
{\rm diam}\,Q\approx S
.

Let us define

G_{R}\coloneqq\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}Q.

The notation
G_{R}
stands for “good” in the following sense:

Lemma 5.18
.

We have
\sigma\left(\bigcup_{Q\in U_{m}(R)}Q\setminus G_{R}\right)=\sigma\left(\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}Q\right)\lesssim\delta^{1/3}\sigma(3R_{W})
.

Proof.

For each
Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)
, let
L_{Q}
be as in (
5.11
) (with
\varepsilon=2k\delta_{\beta}
) and
L_{B_{Q}}
as in (
5.1
). By the same computations in
Remark

5.16
, both
L_{Q}
and
L_{B_{Q}}
satisfy the assumptions of
Lemma

5.5
with
\varepsilon=2k\delta_{\beta}
, and hence we conclude that
\angle(L_{Q},L_{B_{Q}})\lesssim k\delta_{\beta}
. By the same argument,
\angle(L_{R},L_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}})\lesssim k\delta_{\beta}
, where
\mathfrak{B}_{R}
is the ball in (
5.25
) so that
\bigcup_{Q\in U_{m}(R)}Q\subset\mathfrak{B}_{R}
.

From
\angle(L_{Q},L_{B_{Q}})\lesssim k\delta_{\beta}
,
\angle(L_{R},L_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}})\lesssim k\delta_{\beta}
, and the stopping condition
\angle(L_{R},L_{Q})>\alpha
, we get
\angle(L_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}},L_{B_{Q}})>\alpha/2
provided
\delta_{\beta}\ll\alpha/k
, see (
C4
). From this and (
5.2
) we have
\angle(m_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}\nu,m_{B_{Q}}\nu)>\alpha/3
provided
\delta^{\frac{1}{2n}}\ll\alpha
, which is granted by (
C4
). In particular
|m_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}\nu-m_{B_{Q}}\nu|\gtrsim 1-\cos\alpha
by the law of cosines.

Therefore, by Chebysheff’s inequality we have

\displaystyle\sigma\left(\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}Q\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}\sigma(Q)\lesssim\frac{1}{1-\cos\alpha}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}\int_{Q}|m_{B_{Q}}\nu-m_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}\nu|\,d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\cos\alpha}\left(\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}\int_{Q}|\nu-m_{B_{Q}}\nu|\,d\sigma+\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}\int_{Q}|\nu-m_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}\nu|\,d\sigma\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\cos\alpha}\left(\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}\int_{B_{Q}}|\nu-m_{B_{Q}}\nu|\,d\sigma+\int_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|\nu-m_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}\nu|\,d\sigma\right),

where we simply used in the last step that
Q\subset B_{Q}
and
\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}Q\subset\bigcup_{Q\in U_{m}(R)}Q\subset\mathfrak{B}_{R}
. The first term is controlled by

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}\int_{B_{Q}}|\nu-m_{B_{Q}}\nu|\,d\sigma\overset{\text{{(\ref{bmo norm in large scales})}}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\leq}\delta_{*}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}\sigma(B_{Q})\approx\delta_{*}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}\sigma(Q)

\displaystyle=\delta_{*}\sigma\left(\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}Q\right)\leq\delta_{*}\sigma\left(\bigcup_{Q\in U_{m}(R)}Q\right)\lesssim\delta_{*}\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{R}).

The other term is controlled by

\int_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|\nu-m_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}\nu|\,d\sigma\overset{\text{{(\ref{bmo norm in large scales})}}}{\leq}\delta_{*}\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{R}).

All in all, from the last three inline equations, and using
\delta_{*}\leq\delta
,
\delta\leq\alpha^{3}
(see (
C4
)) and
\frac{\delta}{1-\cos(\delta^{1/3})}\leq 3\delta^{1/3}
for small enough
\delta
, we have

\sigma\left(\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{BA}}(R)}Q\right)\lesssim\frac{\delta_{*}}{1-\cos\alpha}\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{R})\lesssim\delta^{1/3}\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{R}).

The lemma follows as
\sigma(3R_{W})\approx_{m}\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{R})
.
∎

Using the set
G_{R}
defined above, we decompose

\sigma(\{x\in R_{W}:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}})(x)>40\lambda,{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\})\leq\boxed{1}+\boxed{2}+\boxed{3},

where

\displaystyle\boxed{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sigma(R_{W}\setminus G_{R}),

\displaystyle\boxed{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sigma(\{x\in G_{R}:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}})(x)>20\lambda,{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\}),\text{ and}

\displaystyle\boxed{3}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sigma(\{x\in\partial\Omega:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\setminus G_{R}})(x)>20\lambda\}).

Using the control of the measure of
\bigcup_{Q\in U_{m}(R)}Q\setminus G_{R}
in
Lemma

5.18
, we estimate
[formula]
and
[formula]
:

Lemma 5.19
.

We have
\boxed{1}\lesssim\delta^{1/3}\sigma(3R_{W})
.

Proof.

This is just by the inclusion
\partial\Omega\cap 3R_{W}\subset mR\subset\bigcup_{Q\in U_{m}(R)}Q
and
Lemma

5.18
.
∎

Lemma 5.20
.

We have
\boxed{3}\lesssim A^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}\delta^{\frac{1}{3}(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\gamma}})}\sigma(3R_{W})
. In particular
\boxed{3}\leq c_{\delta}\sigma(3R_{W})
with
c_{\delta}\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
, by (
C3
).

Proof.

By Chebysheff’s inequality and the fact that
K_{l,*}g\leq K_{*}g
for any
g\in L^{p}(\sigma)
, we have

\boxed{3}\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\int_{\partial\Omega}|K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\setminus G_{R}})|^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}\,d\sigma\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\int_{\partial\Omega}|K_{*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\setminus G_{R}})|^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}\,d\sigma.

Since
L^{p}(\sigma)\subset L^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\sigma)
(as we are assuming that
0<\gamma<p-1
), by (
3.5a
) (since
\partial\Omega
is uniformly rectifiable) and Hölder’s inequality respectively, the right-hand side term is bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\int_{\partial\Omega}|K_{*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\setminus G_{R}})|^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}\,d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\int_{\partial\Omega}|f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\setminus G_{R}}|^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}\,d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma(3R_{W}\setminus G_{R})^{1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}}{\lambda^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\left(\int_{3R_{W}}|f|^{1+\gamma}\,d\sigma\right)^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma(3R_{W}\setminus G_{R})^{1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\sigma(3R_{W})^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}}{\lambda^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{3R_{W}}|f|^{1+\gamma}\,d\sigma\right)^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}.

Using that
\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{3R_{W}}|f|^{1+\gamma}\,d\sigma\right)^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\lesssim{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x_{0})^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x_{0})\leq A\lambda
and
3R_{W}\subset U_{m}(R)
in the latter term, we conclude

\boxed{3}\lesssim A^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}\left(\frac{\sigma\left(\bigcup_{Q\in U_{m}(R)}Q\setminus G_{R}\right)}{\sigma(3R_{W})}\right)^{1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\sigma(3R_{W}),

and the lemma follows by applying
Lemma

5.18
in the last term.
∎

It remains to study the term
\boxed{2}
. As in the terms
[formula]
and
[formula]
, we want to see:

Lemma 5.21
.

We have
\boxed{2}\lesssim(\alpha A^{2}+\delta^{\frac{1-1/\sqrt{1+\gamma}}{4n}}A^{2})\sigma(3R_{W})
. In particular
\boxed{2}\leq c_{\delta}\sigma(3R_{W})
with
c_{\delta}\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
, by (
C3
) and (
C5
).

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of
Lemma

5.21
. By
Proposition

5.9
there exists a Lipschitz graph
\Gamma_{R}
given by
A:L_{R}\to L_{R}^{\perp}
with norm
\leq C\alpha
such that

{\rm dist}(x,(\Pi_{R}(x),A(\Pi_{R}(x))))\leq Ck\delta_{\beta}d_{R}(x)\text{ for all }x\in k_{0}R\supset mR\supset 3R_{W}.

In particular, for
x\in Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)
, if
\widehat{Q}\supset Q
is the parent of
Q
then
\widehat{Q}\in{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)
, and hence

d_{R}(x)=\inf_{Q\in{\mathsf{Tree}}(R)}\{{\rm dist}(x,Q)+{\rm diam}\,Q\}\leq{\rm diam}\,\widehat{Q}\approx{\rm diam}\,Q\approx S

and

{\rm dist}(x,(\Pi_{R}(x),A(\Pi_{R}(x))))\leq Ck\delta_{\beta}d_{R}(x)\lesssim k\delta_{\beta}S.

So, if
\delta_{\beta}\ll 1/k
is small enough we have

\Gamma_{R}\cap\frac{1}{2}B(Q)\not=\emptyset;

see (
5.24
) for the definition of
B(Q)
. This in particular implies

{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(B(Q))\approx\sigma(Q).

Given
f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}\in L^{p}(\sigma)
, let us define an auxiliary function
f_{\Gamma_{R}}
in
\Gamma_{R}
. First, for each
Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)
, let

(5.26)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline oundofthenewfunction}f_{\Gamma_{R}}(Q)\coloneqq\frac{1}{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(\Gamma_{R}\cap B(Q))}\int_{Q}f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)\approx\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{Q}f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(y)\,d\sigma(y),

and we define the function
f_{\Gamma_{R}}
in
\Gamma_{R}
as

(5.27)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline ef:defoff_{g}amma}f_{\Gamma_{R}}(x)\coloneqq\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}f_{\Gamma_{R}}(Q)\mathbf{1}_{B(Q)}(x),\quad x\in\Gamma_{R}.

Note that
f_{\Gamma_{R}}
is a piecewise constant function, since
\{B(Q)\}_{Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}
is a pairwise disjoint family. This follows from the fact that
\{Q\}_{Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}
is a pairwise disjoint family and the choice of
c_{1}
in (
5.24
). By definition of
f_{\Gamma_{R}}
and (
5.25
), we have

(5.28)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline upportoffunctioninlipgraph}\operatorname{supp}f_{\Gamma_{R}}\subset\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\overline{B(Q)}\subset\mathfrak{B}_{R}.

From the definition of
f_{\Gamma_{R}}
from
f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}
in (
5.27
) we have that both
f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap Q}\,d\sigma
and
f_{\Gamma_{R}}\mathbf{1}_{B(Q)}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}
have the same mass for each
Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)
. That is,

(5.29)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline amemass}\int_{\Gamma_{R}}f_{\Gamma_{R}}\mathbf{1}_{B(Q)}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}={\mathcal{H}}^{n}(\Gamma_{R}\cap B(Q))f_{\Gamma_{R}}(Q)=\int_{Q}f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap Q}\,d\sigma.

Before seeing some properties of
f_{\Gamma_{R}}
, let us define the subfamily
I_{R}
of
{\mathsf{SA}}(R)
as

I_{R}\coloneqq\{Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R):\exists x\in Q\text{ with }K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}})(x)>6\lambda\text{ and }{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\}.

For each
Q\in I_{R}
, we fix
z_{Q}\in Q
(do not confuse with
c_{Q}
, the “center” of
Q
) such that

(5.30)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:maximalboundforcubesinIR}{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(z_{Q})\leq A\lambda.

The following lemma shows that
f_{\Gamma_{R}}
inherits a localized maximal bound from
f
, restricted to the cubes
Q\in I_{R}
.

Lemma 5.22
.

For each
Q\in I_{R}
we have

\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.71397pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.05457pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.4484pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.27722pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{R}\cap B(Q)}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}\lesssim A\lambda\quad\text{and}\quad\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.71397pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.05457pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.4484pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.27722pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{R}\cap\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}\lesssim A\lambda.

Proof.

Let
z_{Q}
denote the point in
Q
such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(z_{Q})\leq A\lambda
, see (
5.30
). The first inequality follows from the definition of
f_{\Gamma_{R}}
, (
5.26
) and
Q\in I_{R}
. That is,

\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{R}\cap B(Q)}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y)\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma}}=|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(Q)|\lesssim\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{Q}|f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)\lesssim{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(z_{Q})\leq A\lambda.

Similarly, the second inequality is obtained as

\displaystyle\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{R}\cap\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(\Gamma_{R}\cap\mathfrak{B}_{R})}\int_{\Gamma_{R}\cap\mathfrak{B}_{R}}\left(\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(Q)|\mathbf{1}_{B(Q)}(y)\right)^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y)

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{\ell(R)^{n}}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\int_{\Gamma_{R}\cap B(Q)}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(Q)|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{{(\ref{bound of the new function})}}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\lesssim}\frac{S^{n}}{\ell(R)^{n}}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{Q}|f(y)|\,d\sigma(y)\right)^{1+\gamma}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(Jensen)}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\lesssim}\frac{1}{\ell(R)^{n}}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\int_{Q}|f(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d\sigma(y)\overset{\text{{(\ref{def of ball containing U_m R})}}}{\lesssim}\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|f(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(z_{Q})^{1+\gamma}\leq(A\lambda)^{1+\gamma},

where we took any
z_{Q}
with
Q\in I_{R}
in the last inequality.
∎

To shorten the notation, for
\varepsilon>0
we denote

\Phi_{\varepsilon}(z)\coloneqq\frac{z}{|z|^{n+1}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z|>\varepsilon\}}\text{ for }z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\setminus\{0\}.

We present two lemmas that we will need in the proof of
Lemma

5.21
.

Lemma 5.23
.

For all
x\in Q\in I_{R}
, all
x^{\prime}\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}
and every
\varepsilon\geq T
,

\left|\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)-\int_{\Gamma_{R}}\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y)\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda.

Proof.

We write

\displaystyle\boxed{M}=

\displaystyle\left|\int\left(\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)-\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial\Omega}(\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y))f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)-\int\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\right|

\displaystyle\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\boxed{M1}+\boxed{M2}.

Let us bound the term
\boxed{M1}
. Since
x\in Q
and
x^{\prime}\in B(Q)
satisfy
|x-x^{\prime}|\leq 2{\rm diam}\,Q<2S
, and
\Phi_{\varepsilon}
is the truncated kernel of big scales
\varepsilon\geq T\gg 100S
, in particular
|x-y|\geq T\gg 100S
, we have
|x-x^{\prime}|/|x-y|\leq S/T\leq 1/2
. Thus, we can use the cancellation property of the Calderón-Zygmund kernel (or just the mean value theorem) to obtain

|\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)|\lesssim\frac{|x-x^{\prime}|}{|x-y|^{n+1}}\mathbf{1}_{B(x,\varepsilon/2)^{c}}(y)\lesssim\frac{S}{|x-y|^{n+1}}\mathbf{1}_{B(x,T/2)^{c}}(y),

where we used in the last step that
\varepsilon\geq T
and that
x\in Q
and
x^{\prime}\in B(Q)
satisfy
|x-x^{\prime}|\leq 2S
. From this we get

\displaystyle\boxed{M1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\partial\Omega}|\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)||f(y)|\mathbf{1}_{G_{R}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)\lesssim S\int_{B(x,T/2)^{c}}\frac{|f(y)|}{|x-y|^{n+1}}\,d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle=S\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{B(x,2^{k}T)\setminus B(x,2^{k-1}T)}\frac{|f(y)|}{|x-y|^{n+1}}\,d\sigma(y)\lesssim\frac{S}{T}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x,2^{k}T)}|f|\,d\sigma(y)\overset{\text{{(\ref{eq:maximal bound for cubes in IR})}}}{\lesssim}\frac{S}{T}A\lambda,

where we used in the last step that
Q\in I_{R}
.

It remains to bound the term
\boxed{M2}
. We have

\displaystyle\boxed{M2}=

\displaystyle\left|\int\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)-\int\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\int_{\widetilde{Q}}\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)-\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\int_{B(\widetilde{Q})}\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\left|\int\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap\widetilde{Q}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)-\int\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\mathbf{1}_{B(\widetilde{Q})}(y)\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\right|.

To shorten the notation, we write
\boxed{M2}\leq\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})}
where

\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})}\coloneqq\left|\int\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)\left(f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap\widetilde{Q}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)-f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\mathbf{1}_{B(\widetilde{Q})}(y)\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\right)\right|.

Let us study
\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})}
for each
\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)
. Since we have that
f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap\widetilde{Q}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)-f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\mathbf{1}_{B(\widetilde{Q})}(y)\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)
has zero mass for each
\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)
, see (
5.29
), we can add
\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-c_{\widetilde{Q}})
inside the integral sign in the last term, where
c_{\widetilde{Q}}
is the “center” of
\widetilde{Q}
,
6
6

6

Any point in
\widetilde{Q}
would do the job.
and therefore

\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})}=\left|\int(\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)-\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-c_{\widetilde{Q}}))(f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap\widetilde{Q}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)-f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\mathbf{1}_{B(\widetilde{Q})}(y)\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y))\right|.

With this we have

\displaystyle\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})}\leq

\displaystyle\int|\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)-\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-c_{\widetilde{Q}})||f(y)|\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap\widetilde{Q}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle+\int|\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)-\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-c_{\widetilde{Q}})||f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)|\mathbf{1}_{B(\widetilde{Q})}(y)\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y).

For
y\in\widetilde{Q}
or
y\in B(\widetilde{Q})
, note first that
|y-c_{\widetilde{Q}}|\leq{\rm diam}\,\widetilde{Q}<S
, and second,
|x^{\prime}-y|>\varepsilon\geq T\gg 100S
by the truncation of the kernel
\Phi_{\varepsilon}
. Hence,
|y-c_{\widetilde{Q}}|/|y-x^{\prime}|\leq 1/2
. Therefore, by the cancellation of the Calderón-Zygmund kernel
\Phi_{\varepsilon}
we have

|\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-y)-\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}-c_{\widetilde{Q}})|\lesssim\frac{|y-c_{\widetilde{Q}}|}{|x^{\prime}-y|^{n+1}}\leq\frac{S}{|x^{\prime}-y|^{n+1}}.

Thus, defining

\displaystyle\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\widetilde{Q}\cap\{|y-x^{\prime}|\geq T/2\}}\frac{S}{|x^{\prime}-y|^{n+1}}|f(y)|\,d\sigma(y),\text{ and}

\displaystyle\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})_{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{B(\widetilde{Q})\cap\Gamma_{R}\cap\{|y-x^{\prime}|\geq T/2\}}\frac{S}{|x^{\prime}-y|^{n+1}}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)|\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y).

we have

\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})}\lesssim\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})_{\sigma}}+\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})_{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}}.

Summing
\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})_{\sigma}}
over
\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)
, as we did in the bound of
\boxed{M1}
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq S\int_{B(x^{\prime},T/2)^{c}}\frac{|f(y)|}{|x^{\prime}-y|^{n+1}}\,d\sigma(y)\lesssim\frac{S}{T}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x^{\prime},2^{k}T)}|f|\,d\sigma\overset{\text{{(\ref{eq:maximal bound for cubes in IR})}}}{\lesssim}\frac{S}{T}A\lambda,

where we used in the last step that
Q\in I_{R}
.

We obtain the same bound for
\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})_{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}}
. Indeed, by the same computations above we have

(5.31)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:step1ofboundinlipgraph}\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})_{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}}\lesssim S\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{B(x^{\prime},T/2)^{c}}\frac{|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)|}{|x^{\prime}-y|^{n+1}}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\lesssim\frac{S}{T}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x^{\prime},2^{k}T)}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}|\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}.

For each
k\geq 0
, we define

{\mathsf{SA}}_{k}(R)\coloneqq\{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R):\widetilde{Q}\cap B(x^{\prime},2^{k}T)\not=\emptyset\}.

Since
|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(\widetilde{Q})|{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(B(\widetilde{Q}))\leq\int_{\widetilde{Q}}|f|\,d\sigma
for all
\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)
(see (
5.26
)) and
\widetilde{Q}\subset B(x^{\prime},2^{k+1}T)
if
\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}_{k}(R)
(because
{\rm diam}\,\widetilde{Q}<S\ll T
for all
\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)
), we have

(5.32)

\displaystyle\int_{B(x^{\prime},2^{k}T)}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}|\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}_{k}(R)}\int_{B(\widetilde{Q})}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}|\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}=\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}_{k}(R)}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(\widetilde{Q})|{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(B(\widetilde{Q}))

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}_{k}(R)}\int_{\widetilde{Q}}|f|\,d\sigma\leq\int_{B(x^{\prime},2^{k+1}T)}|f|\,d\sigma.

By (
5.31
) and (
5.32
), using now that
Q\in I_{R}
(i.e., (
5.30
)), we conclude

(5.33)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:step3ofboundinlipgraph}\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})_{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}}\lesssim\frac{S}{T}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x^{\prime},2^{k+1}T)}|f|\,d\sigma\lesssim\frac{S}{T}A\lambda,

as claimed.

All in all, we conclude

\boxed{M}\leq\boxed{M1}+\boxed{M2}\leq C\frac{S}{T}A\lambda\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda,

where we use (
C6
) in the last step.
∎

From now on, we write the double layer potential in
\Gamma_{R}
as
K^{\Gamma_{R}}
, replacing
\partial\Omega
and its surface measure
\sigma
by
\Gamma_{R}
and
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}
in (
3.3
).

The second lemma for the proof of
Lemma

5.21
is about a continuity-type result for the large scale double layer potential in
\Gamma_{R}
.

Lemma 5.24
.

For any
Q\in I_{R}
we have
|K_{l,*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(x)-K_{l,*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(z)|\leq\lambda
for all
x,z\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}
.

Proof.

For
\varepsilon\geq T
, since
x,z\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}
,
{\rm diam}\,Q<S
and
\varepsilon\geq T\gg S
, we have
|x-y|\approx|z-y|
if
|y-x|>\varepsilon
. So, using the cancellation of the Calderón-Zygmund kernel, we have

\displaystyle|K_{\varepsilon}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(x)-K_{\varepsilon}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(z)|

\displaystyle=\left|\int\langle\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\Phi_{\varepsilon}(z-y),\nu_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\rangle f_{\Gamma_{R}}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|y-x|\geq T/2}\frac{|x-z|}{|x-y|^{n+1}}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)|\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)

\displaystyle\leq S\int_{|y-x|\geq T/2}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{n+1}}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)|\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(y).

Note that the last element is in fact
\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\in{\mathsf{SA}}(R)}\boxed{M2(\widetilde{Q})_{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}}
in the proof of
Lemma

5.23
, replacing
x^{\prime}
by
x
. Thus, by the same computations in (
5.31
), (
5.32
) and (
5.33
), we have
|K_{\varepsilon}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(x)-K_{\varepsilon}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(z)|\lesssim\frac{S}{T}A\lambda
. By the condition (
C6
), we conclude that this is bounded by
\leq\lambda
.
∎

For the proof of
Lemma

5.21
, we will compare the double layer potential in
\partial\Omega
with the double layer potential in the Lipschitz graph
\Gamma_{R}
, so that we can use
Lemmas

5.23
and
5.24
, and known properties of
K^{\Gamma_{R}}
. The following is a simplified version of
[
HMT10
, Theorem 4.34]
, see also
[
HMT10
, (4.4.9)]
.

Theorem 5.25
.

Let
\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a Lipschitz function,
\Gamma=\{(x,\varphi(x)):x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\}
its graph, and
1<p<\infty
. Then

(5.34)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:Lpnormdlponlipgraph}\|K_{*}^{\Gamma}\|_{L^{p}(\Gamma)\to L^{p}(\Gamma)}\leq C_{n,p}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}(1+\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})})^{N},

for some
N=N(n)
.

Let us see the proof of
Lemma

5.21
. During the proof,
{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{R}}^{\partial\Omega}
and
{\mathcal{R}}^{\Gamma_{R}}
denote the Riesz transform in
\partial\Omega
and
\Gamma_{R}
respectively, see
Definition

2.16
, and
{\mathcal{R}}_{*}
and
{\mathcal{R}}^{\Gamma_{R}}_{*}
their respective maximal operators as in (
2.9
).

Proof of
Lemma

5.21
.

For each
Q\in I_{R}
, let us study first

\sigma(\{x\in Q:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}})(x)>20\lambda,{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\}).

For any
x\in Q\in I_{R}
and any
z\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}
, we have

\displaystyle K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}})(x)\leq

\displaystyle K_{l,*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(z)+{\mathcal{R}}_{*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(\nu_{\partial\Omega}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(x)

\displaystyle+{\mathcal{R}}_{*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}}(\nu_{\Gamma_{R}}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(z)

\displaystyle+\sup_{\varepsilon\geq T}\left|\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x-\cdot)f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}\,d\sigma-\int_{\Gamma_{R}}\Phi_{\varepsilon}(z-\cdot)f_{\Gamma_{R}}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}\right|,

where
N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}
is the orthogonal unit vector to
L_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}
in (
5.1
) for the ball
\mathfrak{B}_{R}
defined in
5.25
. Therefore, if we fix (once and for all, for the cube
Q
) a point
z(Q)\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}
satisfying

{\mathcal{R}}_{*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}}(\nu_{\Gamma_{R}}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(z(Q))\leq\inf_{z\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}}(\nu_{\Gamma_{R}}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(z)+\frac{\lambda}{2},

then we have that
7
7

7

The reader may notice that the first set on the right-hand side below is either empty or all of
Q
, since
z(Q)
does not depend on
x
. In the latter case we will see that it is nonetheless small, as the continuity-type estimate
Lemma

5.24
reduces it to the infimum of
K_{l,*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})
over
B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}
, which is controlled below.

\sigma(\{x\in Q:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}})(x)>6\lambda\})\leq\,\sigma(\{x\in Q:K_{l,*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(z(Q))>2\lambda\})\\
\begin{aligned} &+\sigma(\{x\in Q:{\mathcal{R}}_{*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(\nu_{\partial\Omega}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(x)>\lambda\})\\
&+\sigma(\{x\in Q:\inf_{z\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}}(\nu_{\Gamma_{R}}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(z)>\lambda\})\\
&\begin{aligned} \,+\,\sigma(\{x\in Q:\sup_{\varepsilon\geq T}\,\biggl|&\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)f(y)\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(y)\,d\sigma(y)\\
&-\int_{\Gamma_{R}}\Phi_{\varepsilon}(z(Q)-y)f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y)\biggr|>\lambda\}).\end{aligned}\end{aligned}

By
Lemma

5.23
, the last term drops out as the corresponding set is empty, and, since
z(Q)\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}
,
Lemma

5.24
implies that the first is controlled by
\sigma(\{x\in Q:\inf_{z\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}}K_{l,*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(z)>\lambda\})
. That is,

\sigma(\{x\in Q:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}})(x)>6\lambda\})\leq\sigma(\{x\in Q:\inf_{z\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}}K_{l,*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(z)>\lambda\})\\
\begin{aligned} &+\sigma(\{x\in Q:{\mathcal{R}}_{*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(\nu_{\partial\Omega}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(x)>\lambda\})\\
&+\sigma(\{x\in Q:\inf_{z\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}}{\mathcal{R}}_{*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}}(\nu_{\Gamma_{R}}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(z)>\lambda\})\\
\eqqcolon\,&\boxed{2_{Q,i}}+\boxed{2_{Q,ii}}+\boxed{2_{Q,iii}}.\end{aligned}

Using the infimum property,
\boxed{2_{Q,i}}
is treated as

\displaystyle\boxed{2_{Q,i}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma(Q)}{\lambda^{1+\gamma}}\left(\inf_{z\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}}K_{l,*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(z)\right)^{1+\gamma}\approx\frac{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma_{R}}(B(Q))}{\lambda^{1+\gamma}}\left(\inf_{z\in B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}}K_{l,*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(z)\right)^{1+\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{1+\gamma}}\int_{B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}}|K_{*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y),

in the last step we removed the restriction at large scales. By the same computations above,

\boxed{2_{Q,iii}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{1+\gamma}}\int_{B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}}|{\mathcal{R}}_{*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}}(\nu_{\Gamma_{R}}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y).

Using Chebysheff’s inequality, the term
\boxed{2_{Q,ii}}
is controlled directly by

\boxed{2_{Q,ii}}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\int_{Q}|{\mathcal{R}}_{*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(\nu_{\partial\Omega}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(y)|^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}\,d\sigma(y).

All in all,

\sigma(\{x\in Q:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}})(x)>6\lambda\})\lesssim\,\frac{1}{\lambda^{1+\gamma}}\int_{B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}}|K_{*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y)\\
\begin{aligned} &+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\int_{Q}|{\mathcal{R}}_{*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(\nu_{\partial\Omega}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(y)|^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}\,d\sigma(y)\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda^{1+\gamma}}\int_{B(Q)\cap\Gamma_{R}}|{\mathcal{R}}_{*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}}(\nu_{\Gamma_{R}}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y).\end{aligned}

Summing over all
Q\in I_{R}\subset{\mathsf{SA}}(R)
and using the above inequality in the last step, we have

\displaystyle\boxed{2}=

\displaystyle\sigma(\{x\in G_{R}:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}})(x)>20\lambda,{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in I_{R}}\sigma(\{x\in Q:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}})(x)>20\lambda,{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(x)\leq A\lambda\})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in I_{R}}\sigma(\{x\in Q:K_{l,*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}})(x)>6\lambda\})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{1+\gamma}}\int_{\Gamma_{R}}|K_{*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}})(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\int_{\partial\Omega}|{\mathcal{R}}_{*}(f\mathbf{1}_{3R_{W}\cap G_{R}}(\nu_{\partial\Omega}(y)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(y)|^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}\,d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\lambda^{1+\gamma}}\int_{\Gamma_{R}}|{\mathcal{R}}_{*}^{\Gamma_{R}}(f_{\Gamma_{R}}(\nu_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}))(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y)

\displaystyle\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\boxed{2_{i}}+\boxed{2_{ii}}+\boxed{2_{iii}}.

The smallness of the term
\boxed{2_{i}}
will come from the
L^{p}(\Gamma_{R})
norm of
K^{\Gamma_{R}}_{*}
in the Lipschitz graph with small constant
\lesssim\alpha
, while we will bound the terms
\boxed{2_{ii}}
and
\boxed{2_{iii}}
using the smallness assumption on the unit normal vectors, after using that the Riesz transform is bounded in
L^{p}
(with constant depending on
p
). We do this in the rest of the proof.

Using that the Lipschitz norm of
\Gamma_{R}
is
\lesssim\alpha
small, by
Theorem

5.25
we have that the
L^{q}(\Gamma_{R})
norm (in every
1<q<\infty
) of
K_{*}^{\Gamma_{R}}
is
\lesssim_{q}\alpha
, and hence we get

\boxed{2_{i}}\overset{\text{{(\ref{eq:Lp norm dlp on lip graph})}}}{\lesssim}\frac{\alpha^{1+\gamma}}{\lambda^{1+\gamma}}\int_{\Gamma_{R}}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y)\approx\frac{\alpha^{1+\gamma}}{\lambda^{1+\gamma}}\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{R})\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{R}\cap\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|f_{\Gamma_{R}}(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(y),

where we used in the last step that
\operatorname{supp}f_{\Gamma_{R}}\subset\mathfrak{B}_{R}
, see (
5.28
), and we allow the implicit constant to depend on
\gamma
. By
Lemma

5.22
, the last averaged integral is controlled by
\lesssim(A\lambda)^{1+\gamma}
and hence

\boxed{2_{i}}\lesssim\alpha^{1+\gamma}A^{1+\gamma}\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{R})\leq\alpha A^{2}\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{R})\approx\alpha A^{2}\sigma(3R_{W}),

where the second-to-last inequality is simply because
0<\gamma<1
.

Using that the Riesz transform is bounded on UR sets, Hölder’s inequality, and that there exists some point
z\in 3R_{W}\subset\mathfrak{B}_{R}
such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{1+\gamma}f(z)\leq A\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\boxed{2_{ii}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\int_{3R_{W}}(|f(y)||\nu_{\partial\Omega}(y)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|)^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}\,d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\sigma(3R_{W})\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|f(y)|^{1+\gamma}\,d\sigma(y)\right)^{1/\sqrt{1+\gamma}}\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|\nu_{\partial\Omega}(y)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|\,d\sigma(y)\right)^{1-1/\sqrt{1+\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}}\sigma(3R_{W})(A\lambda)^{\sqrt{1+\gamma}}\left(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|\nu_{\partial\Omega}(y)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|\,d\sigma(y)\right)^{1-1/\sqrt{1+\gamma}}.

In the second step we also used that
|\nu_{\partial\Omega}(\cdot)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|\leq 2

\sigma
-a.e. to reduce the exponent of
|\nu_{\partial\Omega}(y)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|
to
1
(as in (
4.3
)). Using the smallness assumption of the oscillation of the unit normal vector in (
3.8
) and that
|m_{\sigma,\mathfrak{B}_{R}}\nu_{\partial\Omega}-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|\lesssim\delta^{\frac{1}{4n}}
by (
5.3
), we have

\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|\nu_{\partial\Omega}(y)-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|\,d\sigma(y)\leq\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|\nu_{\partial\Omega}(y)-m_{\sigma,\mathfrak{B}_{R}}\nu_{\partial\Omega}|\,d\sigma(y)+|m_{\sigma,\mathfrak{B}_{R}}\nu_{\partial\Omega}-N_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}|\lesssim\delta^{\frac{1}{4n}}.

We conclude then that

\boxed{2_{ii}}\leq C\delta_{*}^{\frac{1-1/\sqrt{1+\gamma}}{4n}}A^{2}\sigma(3R_{W}).

The term
\boxed{2_{iii}}
is treated similarly, using the
L^{\infty}
bound of the oscillation of the unit vector as
\Gamma_{R}
is a Lipschitz graph of norm
\lesssim\alpha
. That is, since
\Gamma_{R}
is the Lipschitz graph of
A:L_{R}\to L_{R}^{\perp}
in
Proposition

5.9
with norm
\lesssim\alpha
, then
\angle(\nu_{\Gamma_{R}}(y),N_{R})\lesssim\alpha
for all
y\in\Gamma_{R}
, where
N_{R}\perp L_{R}
, and on the other hand, since
{\rm dist}(x,L_{R})\leq 2k\delta_{\beta}{\rm diam}\,R
for all
x\in kR\supset R
by (
5.11
) and
Remark

5.16
, and
{\rm dist}(x,L_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}})\leq 2\delta_{\beta}r_{\mathfrak{B}_{R}}
for all
x\in\partial\Omega\cap\mathfrak{B}_{R}\supset R
by (
5.1
), by
Lemma

5.5
